# Literature and Novelty Check

Checked on 2026-08-02.  The arXiv searches below used the public Atom API
endpoint `https://export.arxiv.org/api/query`; DOI metadata was checked against
the Crossref REST API.  The purpose of the search was to delimit the theorem
claims, not to infer novelty from a null keyword search.

## arXiv API queries

The following literal `search_query` values were sent to the API (20 results
maximum per query):

- `all:"context-free" AND all:prime AND all:language`: four returned records,
  none on prime numeration languages.  The only mathematically related record
  was Borchert--Rampersad, *Inverse Star, Borders, and Palstars*,
  arXiv:1008.2440; it does not concern prime recognizability.
- `all:Pisot AND all:numeration AND all:automata`: eight returned records,
  including Carton--Couvreur--Delacourt--Ollinger,
  *Linear Recurrence Sequence Automata and the Addition of Abstract Numeration
  Systems*, arXiv:2406.09868 (conference DOI
  10.1007/978-3-031-97548-6_7); Charlier--Cisternino--Stipulanti,
  *Robustness of Pisot-regular sequences*, arXiv:2006.11126,
  DOI 10.1016/j.aam.2020.102151; Massuir--Peltomaki--Rigo,
  *Automatic sequences based on Parry or Bertrand numeration systems*,
  arXiv:1810.11081, DOI 10.1016/j.aam.2019.03.003; and
  Frougny--Steiner, *Minimal weight expansions in Pisot bases*,
  arXiv:0803.2874, DOI 10.1515/JMC.2008.017.  None of the returned records
  states prime-language REG- or CF-immunity.
- `all:"prime language" AND all:automata`: no returned records.
- `all:"abstract numeration systems" AND all:prime`: no returned records.
- `all:Ogden AND all:"interchange lemma"`: no returned records.  These are
  pre-arXiv classical papers and are cited by DOI below.
- Exact-title queries confirmed Dubbe, *The automaticity of the set of
  primes*, arXiv:2409.04314, DOI 10.1016/j.tcs.2025.115480, and Yuen,
  *Kleene Star of the Primes is not Regular in Any Base*, arXiv:2203.16088.

The arXiv API search is negative evidence only.  In particular, the older
JACM papers settle a stronger fixed-base question than their titles alone
suggest, so the paper does not advertise fixed-base CF-immunity as new.

## Exact classical citations

- William Ogden, "A helpful result for proving inherent ambiguity,"
  *Mathematical Systems Theory* 2 (1968), 191--194,
  DOI 10.1007/BF01694004.  This is the marked-position pumping lemma now
  called Ogden's lemma.
- William Ogden, Rockford J. Ross, and Karl Winklmann, "An 'Interchange
  Lemma' for Context-Free Languages," *SIAM Journal on Computing* 14 (1985),
  410--415, DOI 10.1137/0214031.  The present proof does not claim or reproduce
  their interchange lemma; ordinary context-free pumping suffices after a
  fixed-prefix quotient, while Ogden's lemma gives an alternative way to
  localize pumping beyond an eventual-recurrence transient.
- J. Hartmanis and H. Shank, "On the Recognition of Primes by Automata,"
  *Journal of the ACM* 15 (1968), 382--389,
  DOI 10.1145/321466.321470.
- M. P. Schutzenberger, "A Remark on Acceptable Sets of Numbers,"
  *Journal of the ACM* 15 (1968), 300--303,
  DOI 10.1145/321450.321461.  Contemporary citation records describe the
  Hartmanis--Shank and Schutzenberger conclusion as: no infinite set of
  base-`b` primes is recognized by a finite automaton or a pushdown automaton.
  Thus fixed-base prime CF-immunity is prior art.
- M. Minsky and S. Papert, "Unrecognizable Sets of Numbers,"
  *Journal of the ACM* 13 (1966), 281--286,
  DOI 10.1145/321328.321337.
- D. P. Allen, Jr., "On a characterization of the nonregular set of primes,"
  *Journal of Computer and System Sciences* 2 (1968), 464--467,
  DOI 10.1016/S0022-0000(68)80038-8.

## Pisot and numeration references

- Christiane Frougny, "Representations of numbers and finite automata,"
  *Mathematical Systems Theory* 25 (1992), 37--60,
  DOI 10.1007/BF01368783.  This is the normalization/finite-transducer source
  used for linear numeration systems whose characteristic polynomial has the
  Pisot property.
- Veronique Bruyere and Georges Hansel, "Bertrand numeration systems and
  recognizability," *Theoretical Computer Science* 181 (1997), 17--43,
  DOI 10.1016/S0304-3975(96)00260-5.
- Christiane Frougny and Boris Solomyak, "Finite beta-expansions,"
  *Ergodic Theory and Dynamical Systems* 12 (1992), 713--723,
  DOI 10.1017/S0143385700007057.
- Stephane Fabre, "Substitutions et beta-systemes de numeration,"
  *Theoretical Computer Science* 137 (1995), 219--236,
  DOI 10.1016/0304-3975(95)91132-A.
- P. B. A. Lecomte and M. Rigo, "Numeration Systems on a Regular Language,"
  *Theory of Computing Systems* 34 (2001), 27--44,
  DOI 10.1007/S002240010014.  This is cited to distinguish abstract
  numeration systems from the narrower linear Pisot `U`-systems proved here.

## Novelty boundary used in the manuscript

The following assertions are treated as new only relative to the literature
located above and are stated with their full hypotheses:

1. The two-pump block-action congruence modulo the square of the represented
   value and the resulting coprime-quotient chain for an arbitrary
   context-free sublanguage of canonical Zeckendorf representations.
2. CF-immunity of the Zeckendorf prime language, and the stronger unbounded
   distinct-prime-divisor consequences for context-free Zeckendorf
   sublanguages.
3. Prime REG- and CF-immunity for the explicitly defined class of positional
   linear Pisot `U`-systems: integer weights, finite digit alphabet, injective
   canonical representations ordered by length, and an eventual integral
   recurrence with nonzero trailing coefficient.  The proof uses only the
   recurrence block action; Frougny normalization supplies the standard
   finite-state canonical syntax/normalization interface for Pisot
   `U`-systems.

No claim is made for every object sometimes called a "Pisot numeration
system."  In particular, arbitrary real-base beta-shifts, non-positional
abstract numeration systems, systems without an eventual integral recurrence,
and non-injective redundant representations are explicit open interfaces.
The Pisot-unit hypothesis is needed for the unrestricted deep-quotient and
induced-tree conclusions and for unconditional bounded-omega immunity. For a
nonunit recurrence, primes dividing the trailing coefficient make the
companion action singular modulo some composite values. The local-adic
dichotomy nevertheless gives prime and bounded-Omega MCF-immunity; bounded
distinct-prime support can escape only through unbounded valuation at a prime
dividing the trailing coefficient.

## Multiple-context-free and adic extension (2026-08-03)

The public arXiv Atom endpoint at https://export.arxiv.org/api/query was queried
with the following literal searches (and with a single-record
search_query=id:2509.02117 control):

- all:"multiple context-free" AND all:pumping;
- all:"multiple context-free" AND all:prime;
- all:"adic topology" AND all:integers;
- all:"Cantor-Bendixson" AND all:"prime factors";
- all:"divisibility tree" AND all:language.

Every API request returned HTTP 429 after retries with an identifying user
agent and four-second spacing. These failures are not treated as null search
results. The corresponding arXiv web searches, Crossref bibliographic
searches, DBLP metadata, and exact-source checks located no paper asserting an
MCFL prime-immunity theorem for recurrence numeration, an MCFL-induced local
congruence orbit, or the induced divisibility-tree conclusion.

The following published inputs delimit what is not new:

- H. Seki, T. Matsumura, M. Fujii, and T. Kasami, "On multiple
  context-free grammars," *Theoretical Computer Science* 88 (1991),
  191--229, DOI 10.1016/0304-3975(91)90374-B. Its Lemma 3.2 is the weak
  pumping lemma supplying one synchronized family with 2k pumped factors.
- M. Kanazawa, G. M. Kobele, J. Michaelis, S. Salvati, and R. Yoshinaka,
  "The Failure of the Strong Pumping Lemma for Multiple Context-Free
  Languages," *Theory of Computing Systems* 55 (2014), 250--278,
  DOI 10.1007/S00224-014-9534-Z.
- A. Duncan, M. Elder, L. Frenkel, and M. Liu, "A substitution lemma for
  multiple context-free languages," *International Journal of Algebra and
  Computation* (2026), DOI 10.1142/S0218196726500463,
  arXiv:2509.02117. The direct arXiv PDF was checked; its Lemma 5.3 quotes
  the exact Seki weak-pumping factorization used in the manuscript.
- K. A. Broughan, "Adic Topologies for the Rational Integers," *Canadian
  Journal of Mathematics* 55 (2003), 711--723,
  DOI 10.4153/CJM-2003-030-3. The Cambridge primary PDF was checked.
  Theorem 2.4 gives the rational-space classification, Corollary 3.1 gives
  the Cantor completion, and Theorem 4.3 treats full-adic prime-factor
  strata counted with multiplicity.

Accordingly, the manuscript cites the Seki pumping family and Broughan's
topological classifications as inputs. The claims retained as new are the
combination with a fixed recurrence block orbit, the deleted-prime intrinsic
Cantor--Bendixson rank for distinct prime support with bounded local
valuations, the nonunit escape dichotomy and MCF-immunity consequences, and
the unit-system arbitrary-depth quotient and induced-tree constructions.

content.

support, and an exact geometric numerical ray. This is negative novelty
evidence only.

Two published inputs delimit the claim. Seki--Matsumura--Fujii--Kasami,
*Theoretical Computer Science* 88 (1991), 191--229, supply the weak
synchronized pumping family. J.-H. Evertse, "On sums of \(S\)-units and
linear recurrences," *Compositio Mathematica* 53 (1984), 225--244,
Theorem 3, proves unbounded prime-ideal support in ratios of terms of a
nondegenerate recurrence with at least two characteristic roots. The latter
excludes a fixed-\(S\)-unit nondegenerate subsequence; Schur's polynomial
prime-divisor theorem handles the remaining one-root polynomial factor.
Neither published input states the manuscript's equivalence with the
existence of a finite-fan-out synchronized MCFG ray, nor the resulting
positive-semidecidability statement.

# Stage A Theorem Inventory

Paper: `2026_prime_languages_finite_state_obstructions_monatshefte`

Target journal: Monatshefte fuer Mathematik

This inventory is scope-bound to `scope_contract.md`, `scope_contract.json`, `research_directive.md`, and the current TeX sources. It does not edit manuscript TeX.

## Scope Verdict

The main in-scope theorem chain is present.

The base-b side contains the deterministic finite automaton definition, the classical residue-class density dichotomy, explicit PNT input, base-b prime-slice counts under the MSD-first nonzero-leading-digit convention, the quantitative recall/precision obstruction, the symmetric-difference lower bound, and the fixed-DFA recall/precision dichotomy.

The trim-presentation side contains the trim deterministic presentation interface, periodic growth for trim strongly connected presentations, the background relative-density theorem, and the finite-budget corollary used for Zeckendorf slices.

The Zeckendorf side contains the intended primary theorem package. The primary anchor is `thm:coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping`, supported by `lem:zeckendorf-unimodular-block-action` and `lem:zeckendorf-pumping-congruence`. Its consequences are present: strict divisibility chains, new rational prime divisors, unbounded `omega`, unbounded `Omega`, divisibility-antichain obstruction, bounded-distinct-prime REG-immunity, finite-prime-support REG-immunity, bounded-almost-prime REG-immunity, Zeckendorf-Schutzenberger regular immunity, prime-language REG-immunity, nonregularity, and the eventual-sofic exclusion.

## Primary In-Scope Results

- `thm:euler-product-natural-boundary` at `sec_analytic.tex:13`: present, but too weak for the contract as written. It cites Estermann directly rather than giving the required logarithmic-derivative proof route. The source scan did not find the required `leanverified` marker or Lean theorem anchor.

It should be removed from the main spine or split into a separate reducible-presentation paper. The current abstract foregrounds it, which is a style/scope problem.
- `thm:effective-fixed-dfa-prime-obstruction` and `lem:effective-spectral-constants`: candidate split package on certified constants and exact residue-class proof objects for fixed-DFA prime-slice obstruction.

## Naive Truncation Risks

No cross-resolution invariant by naive truncation was found in the labelled theorem-like environments. The multicomponent theorem is a scope-inflation issue, not a fold/truncation invariant issue.

## Journal Style Gaps

- The abstract currently advertises multicomponent finite-budget theory, contrary to the exact scope.
- `thm:euler-product-natural-boundary` lacks the visible Lean marker required by the scope contract.
- `thm:parry-density` should remain background conditional-density packaging unless a human editorial decision promotes it.

## Theorem Inventory

### Block A: Probabilistic (DFA density)

- `thm:dfa-density-dichotomy`: Accepted-word density is eventually periodic mod p up to O(theta^m); either exponentially sparse or positive-density along a residue class
- `cor:dfa-prime-symmetric-diff`: Every DFA language has symmetric difference >= c 2^m/m with binary prime language
- `cor:dfa-prime-recall-precision`: No fixed DFA achieves both recall and precision bounded away from 0 on binary primes

### Block B: Combinatorial (Zeckendorf)

- `thm:zeckendorf-regular-powerlaw`: Regular sublanguages of Zeckendorf language have counting function T^{alpha+o(1)}
- `cor:zeckendorf-prime-language-not-regular`: Language of Zeckendorf representations of primes is not regular
- `prop:sofic-counts-exponential-polynomial`: Sofic shift word counts are exponential polynomials (cited-standard, Lind-Marcus)
- `thm:zeckendorf-primes-not-sofic`: Length slices of prime Zeckendorf language cannot be admissible words of any sofic shift

### Block C: Analytic

- `thm:euler-product-natural-boundary`: Euler product with infinitely many prime-indexed positive factors has |z|=1 as natural boundary
- `prop:finite-zeta-imaginary-periodicity`: det(I - e^{-s}M)^{-1} is 2pi i-periodic; Riemann zeta is not

## Stage Notes

### P2 Research Extension

**Assessment:** Results range from folklore to mild extensions of standard material. The value proposition is systematic assembly across three mathematical perspectives (automata theory, combinatorics on words, analytic number theory), not any single deep theorem.

**Novelty by result:**
- Thm 1 (density dichotomy): Folklore in automata theory (finite Markov chain consequence)
- Cor 2-3 (binary primes): Explicit formulations appear new; underlying fact is implicit in automatic-sequences literature
- Thm 4 (Zeckendorf regular power law): Standard via numeration-system theory (Frougny, Bruyere-Hansel)
- Cor 5 (prime Zeckendorf not regular): Folklore consequence of PNT + growth theory
- Prop 6 (sofic counts): Textbook (Lind-Marcus)
- Thm 7 (primes not sofic): Straightforward application of exponential-polynomial structure
- Thm 8 (natural boundary): Classical technique (Estermann 1928); specific Euler-product formulation may be new
- Prop 9 (periodicity rigidity): Elementary; clean formulation

**Scope decisions:** Paper restricts to complete DFA, binary alphabet, canonical Zeckendorf, non-negative Euler exponents. These restrictions simplify proofs but limit generality. Zeckendorf argument extends to all Parry numeration systems at no extra cost -- should be stated.

**Gaps identified:**
1. DFA definition does not address leading-zeros convention for binary numbers
2. PNT application uses asymptotic notation without specifying implied constants
3. Thm 4 proof: lambda=0 case (finite language) needs separate handling
4. Thm 8 non-cancellation argument too terse; needs explicit clarification or logarithmic approach
5. Prop 9 implicitly requires irrationality of log 2
6. No computational/reproducibility component (purely theoretical)

**Missing references:** Allouche-Shallit (2003), Cobham (1972), Mauduit-Rivat (2010), Bruyere-Hansel (1997), Hardy-Wright, Estermann (1928), Flajolet-Sedgewick (2009), Frougny (1992).

  infinite:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item the prime representation language \(\mathcal P_U\);
    \item every language on which \(\Omega(\val_U(w))\) is bounded;
    \item every finite-prime-support language on which all
    \(v_p(\val_U(w))\), \(p\mid a_0\), are bounded.
  \end{enumerate}
  In a unit system, every bounded-\(\omega(\val_U(w))\) language is
  MCF-immune.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  If neither alternative held, finiteness of \(S\) would give
  \(K\ge0\) and \(E\in\NN_0^S\) such that
  \[
    \{\val_U(w):w\in L\}\subseteq X_{K,E}.
  \]
  By \Cref{prop:local-prime-factor-cb-rank}, \(X_{K,E}\) is scattered.
  This contradicts
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-local-adic-fingerprint}. Prime values satisfy
  \(\omega_{S^c}\le1\) and \(v_p\le1\); bounded \(\Omega\) bounds both
  alternatives; and finite prime support bounds \(\omega_{S^c}\), with
  the remaining valuations bounded by hypothesis. If \(S=\varnothing\),
  the second alternative is absent, giving the unit-system assertion.
\end{proof}

The second alternative is necessary. In ordinary base \(b\), where
\(a_0=b\), the regular LSD-first language
\(\{0^n1:n\ge0\}\) represents \(b^n\). Its distinct prime support is
fixed, while \(v_p(b^n)\) is unbounded for every \(p\mid b\).

\begin{proposition}[Recurrence-witness inflation and strict separation]
  \label{prop:recurrence-witness-separation}
  If a tail has minimal recurrence polynomial \(\mu_U\), then for every
  prime \(p\) it also has a recurrence witness with characteristic
  polynomial \((X-p)\mu_U(X)\).  When \(\abs{\mu_U(0)}=1\), the trailing
  coefficient of this witness has absolute value \(p\).

  Moreover, there are two canonical eventually recurrent
  numeration systems with regular representation languages and recurrence
  witnesses whose trailing-coefficient prime set is \(\{p\}\), but with
  opposite bounded-outside-prime escape behavior:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item ordinary base \(p\) contains the infinite regular language
    \(\{0^n1:n\ge0\}\), whose values are \(p^n\);
    \item the Zeckendorf system, equipped with the order-three recurrence
    witness
    \[
      F_{n+3}=-pF_n+(1-p)F_{n+1}+(p+1)F_{n+2},
    \]
    contains no infinite MCFL \(L\) for which
    \[
      \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{\{p\}^c}(\val(w))<\infty.
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
  Thus the prime divisors of an arbitrarily selected trailing coefficient
  do not determine the escape behavior of the numeration system.
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
  The polynomial identity \(\mu_U(C)z_J=0\) remains valid after
  multiplication by \(X-p\).  In the companion convention, the trailing
  recurrence coefficient is the negative of the constant term of the
  characteristic polynomial, proving the first assertion.

  The base-\(p\) assertion is the preceding example.  For the Zeckendorf
  sequence, multiplication of its minimal polynomial by \(X-p\) gives
  \[
    (X-p)(X^2-X-1)
      =X^3-(p+1)X^2+(p-1)X+p,
  \]
  which is exactly the displayed recurrence and has trailing coefficient
  \(-p\).  This changes neither the sequence nor its regular canonical
  representation language.  On the other hand, its minimal recurrence
  has unit trailing coefficient.  If the displayed supremum were bounded
  by \(K\), then
  \(\omega(\val(w))\le K+1\) on \(L\), contrary to the unit-recurrence
  conclusion of
  \Cref{thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy}.  This proves the separation.
\end{proof}

Let
\[
  \mu_U(X)=X^{d_{\mathrm r}}-c_{d_{\mathrm r}-1}X^{d_{\mathrm r}-1}
    -\cdots-c_1X-c_0
\]
be the minimal recurrence polynomial of a tail of \(U\). Since it
divides the characteristic polynomial of a recurrence allowed in
\textup{(U4)}, its constant term is nonzero. Enlarge the recurrence
threshold to \(J_{\mathrm r}\) so that this minimal recurrence holds for
every \(n\ge J_{\mathrm r}\), and put
\[
  S_{\mathrm r}=\{p:p\text{ is prime and }p\mid c_0\}.
\]

\begin{definition}[Geometric synchronized scheme]
  \label{def:geometric-synchronized-scheme}
  A geometric synchronized scheme for \(U\) consists of
  \(a\in A^{J_{\mathrm r}}\), an integer \(k\ge1\), integers
  \(c\ge1,b\ge2\), and words
  \[
    u_1,\ldots,u_{k+1},\quad
    v_1,\ldots,v_k,\quad w_1,\ldots,w_k,\quad
    s_1,\ldots,s_k\in A^\ast,
  \]
  such that
  \[
    W(t)=a u_1v_1^tw_1s_1^tu_2\cdots
      u_kv_k^tw_ks_k^tu_{k+1}\in\mathcal R_U
      \qquad(t\ge0),
  \]
  \[
    \sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0,
    \qquad \val_U(W(t))=cb^t\quad(t\ge0).
  \]
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Increasing \(S\)-unit recurrences]
  \label{lem:s-unit-increasing-recurrence-rigidity}
  Let \(Y=(Y(n))_{n\ge0}\) be a strictly increasing sequence of positive
  rational integers satisfying a linear recurrence. If all prime
  divisors of all \(Y(n)\) belong to one finite set, then there are
  integers \(r\ge0,h\ge1,c\ge1\), and \(b\ge2\) such that
  \[
    Y(r+hn)=cb^n\qquad(n\ge0).
  \]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  Discard a finite initial segment and choose \(h_0\ge1\) divisible by
  the orders of all root-of-unity quotients between distinct nonzero
  characteristic roots. For each residue class modulo \(h_0\), combine
  terms whose roots acquire the same \(h_0\)-th power. Each resulting
  arithmetic subsequence is nondegenerate.

  If one of these subsequences had at least two characteristic roots,
  Evertse's prime-ideal theorem for quotients of terms of a nondegenerate
  recurrence \cite[Theorem~3]{Evertse1984} would force prime ideals of
  unbounded norm to occur in its term ratios. Here every such ratio is
  supported over the fixed finite set of rational primes, a
  contradiction. Thus any nonzero residue subsequence has the form
  \[
    Z(n)=f(n)\lambda^n
  \]
  with one characteristic root.

  The subsequence is rational-valued and satisfies a recurrence over
  \(\mathbb Q\). Its minimal annihilating polynomial over \(\mathbb Q\)
  has no root other than \(\lambda\). Galois invariance therefore gives
  \(\lambda\in\mathbb Q\), and interpolation gives
  \(f\in\mathbb Q[X]\). If \(f\) were nonconstant, clear its
  denominators to obtain a nonconstant \(g\in\mathbb Z[X]\). The
  identity \(Z(n)=f(n)\lambda^n\) would confine every prime divisor of
  every nonzero \(g(n)\) to a fixed finite set. Indeed, if
  \(\lambda=a/b\) is in lowest terms and \(g=Df\), then
  \[
    g(n)a^n=Db^nZ(n).
  \]
  Thus that fixed set is the union of the prescribed prime set and the
  prime divisors of \(Dab\). This contradicts Schur's theorem that a
  nonconstant integer polynomial has values divisible by infinitely
  many primes; see, for example, \cite{HardyWright2008}. Hence \(f\) is
  constant.

  After a further shift, write \(Z(n)=c\lambda^n\), where
  \(c=Z(0)\) is a positive integer. If \(\lambda=a/b\) in lowest terms,
  integrality for every \(n\) forces \(b^n\mid c\) for every \(n\), so
  \(b=1\). Positivity and strict increase then force
  \(\lambda\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\), proving the assertion.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Geometric-ray characterization]
  \label{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}
  For a canonical eventually recurrent numeration system satisfying
  \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, the following are equivalent.
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item There are a finite \(k\) and an infinite \(k\)-MCFL
    \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) such that
    \[
      \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(\val_U(w))<\infty.
    \]
    \item The system admits a geometric synchronized scheme in the
    sense of \Cref{def:geometric-synchronized-scheme}.
    \item The language \(\mathcal R_U\) contains an infinite linear MCFL
    \(L_0=\{W(t):t\ge0\}\) of finite fan-out for which there are
    \(c\ge1,b\ge2\) satisfying
    \[
      \val_U(W(t))=cb^t\qquad(t\ge0).
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Suppose first that \textup{(i)} holds. Use the minimal tail recurrence
  as the witness in
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, and write its resulting
  ray and strictly increasing value sequence as \(W(t)\) and \(N(t)\).
  Let
  \[
    K_0=\max_{t\ge0}\omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(N(t)),
  \]
  and choose \(t_0\) attaining the maximum. Let \(T\) be the set of
  primes outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\) that divide \(N(t_0)\), and put
  \(q=\prod_{\ell\in T}\ell\). If \(T\ne\varnothing\), take the return
  time \(H=H(q)\) from
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}; if \(T=\varnothing\),
  put \(H=1\). Every prime in \(T\) divides every \(N(t_0+nH)\). A prime
  divisor outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\cup T\) would therefore give at least
  \(K_0+1\) distinct prime divisors outside \(S_{\mathrm r}\), contrary
  to the definition of \(K_0\). Thus all terms of
  \[
    Y(n)=N(t_0+nH)
  \]
  are supported on the fixed finite set \(S_{\mathrm r}\cup T\).

  The entries of every matrix power \(M^n\) satisfy a linear recurrence
  by Cayley--Hamilton. The expression for \(N(t)\) is a finite sum of
  finite products of such entries, and linear recurrence sequences are
  closed under sums and termwise products. Hence \(Y\) is a strictly
  increasing positive integer recurrence. By

 succeeded in 206ms:
  \Cref{lem:s-unit-increasing-recurrence-rigidity}, there are
  \(r\ge0,h\ge1,c\ge1,b\ge2\) such that
  \(N(t_\ast+h_\ast n)=cb^n\), where
  \(t_\ast=t_0+Hr\) and \(h_\ast=Hh\).

  In the synchronized factorization, replace
  \[
    u_j\ \text{by}\ u_jv_j^{t_\ast},\qquad
    v_j\ \text{by}\ v_j^{h_\ast},\qquad
    w_j\ \text{by}\ w_js_j^{t_\ast},\qquad
    s_j\ \text{by}\ s_j^{h_\ast}.
  \]
  This absorbs the fixed powers and parametrizes exactly the subray
  \(W(t_\ast+h_\ast n)\). Its total pumped length remains positive, so
  it is a geometric synchronized scheme. This proves
  \textup{(i)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(ii)}.

  The implication \textup{(ii)}\(\Rightarrow\)\textup{(iii)} is
  \Cref{lem:synchronized-ray-linear-mcfg}. Finally, under
  \textup{(iii)}, the language \(L_0\) is an infinite MCFL and
  \[
    \omega_{S_{\mathrm r}^c}(cb^t)\le\omega(cb)
    \qquad(t\ge0),
  \]
  proving \textup{(i)}.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Positive semidecidability]
  \label{cor:bounded-outside-support-semidecidable}
  Suppose the numeration system is given effectively by its finite
  digit alphabet, recurrence and initial data, and a DFA recognizing
  \(\mathcal R_U\), with the minimal tail recurrence and a valid
  threshold \(J_{\mathrm r}\) effectively computable from these data.
  Then the property in
  \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}\textup{(i)} is
  recursively enumerable, equivalently it has a \(\Sigma^0_1\) upper
  bound.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
  Enumerate \(k\ge1\), \(a\in A^{J_{\mathrm r}}\), and all finite tuples
  of words occurring in
  \Cref{def:geometric-synchronized-scheme}, rejecting tuples of zero
  total pumped length. For a fixed tuple, whether the DFA accepts
  \(W(t)\) for every \(t\ge0\) is decidable: the tuple of powers of the
  finitely many pumped-block transformations is eventually periodic in
  the finite transition monoid, and its preperiod and period are found
  by exhaustive iteration.

  The affine matrices construct an exact linear recurrence for
  \[
    N(t)=\val_U(W(t)).
  \]
  Kronecker products implement the termwise
  products arising from the several synchronized block powers. For a
  tuple accepted at every parameter, compute \(c=N(0)\). The only
  possible integer ratio is \(b=N(1)/N(0)\). Check that
  \(b\in\mathbb Z_{\ge2}\), and then decide whether the recurrence
  \(N(t+1)-bN(t)\) is identically zero by checking an initial segment of
  length equal to an explicitly constructed recurrence order. Thus each
  candidate scheme is decidable, and the enumeration halts exactly on
  positive instances by
  \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}. No claim of a
  decision procedure for negative instances is made.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Arbitrarily deep quotient congruences]
  \label{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain}
  Suppose \(\abs{a_0}=1\), and let \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) be an
  infinite MCFL. For arbitrary sequences \(M_i\ge1\) and \(r_i\ge1\),
  \(L\) contains a strictly increasing sequence \(N_i\) such that
  \[
    N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i,\qquad Q_i>1,\qquad
    Q_i\equiv1\pmod {M_iN_i^{r_i}}.
  \]
  If \(M_i\) is divisible by every prime at most \(B_i\), then every
  prime divisor of \(Q_i\) exceeds \(B_i\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Take the fixed orbit \(N(t)\) from
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, discarding finitely many
  initial parameters so that \(N(t)\ge2\). Having selected
  \(N_i=N(t_i)\), apply the synchronized congruence with
  \[
    q_i=M_iN_i^{r_i+1}.
  \]
  Set \(t_{i+1}=t_i+H(q_i)\). Strict increase and the congruence give
  \[
    N_{i+1}=N_i+c_iM_iN_i^{r_i+1}
           =N_i(1+c_iM_iN_i^{r_i})
  \]
  for an integer \(c_i\ge1\). This proves the first assertion. Under
  the last hypothesis, \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod p\) for every prime
  \(p\le B_i\), so none of those primes divides \(Q_i\).
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Induced full divisibility tree]
  \label{thm:unit-mcfl-divisibility-tree}
  Under the hypotheses of
  \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain}, there is an injection
  \[
    \Phi:\NN^{<\omega}\longrightarrow L
  \]
  such that
  \[
    \val_U(\Phi(\sigma))\mid\val_U(\Phi(\tau))
      \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \sigma\preceq\tau,
  \]
  where \(\preceq\) denotes the prefix order. The quotients attached to
  distinct edges can be chosen pairwise coprime. Moreover, given an
  arbitrary prescribed threshold for each edge, every prime divisor of
  its quotient can be required to exceed that threshold.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Use the fixed strictly increasing orbit from
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}. Enumerate the edges of
  \(\NN^{<\omega}\) so that each parent occurs before its children. Map
  the root to any orbit word of value \(R_0\ge2\). Suppose finitely many
  edges have been assigned, and consider a new edge from a parent of
  value \(P\). Let \(R\) be divisible by \(R_0\), by every previously
  assigned edge quotient, and by every prime not exceeding the prescribed
  threshold for the new edge. Apply the synchronized orbit congruence
  modulo \(P^2R\) at the parameter of the parent. A sufficiently large
  positive multiple of the return time gives an unused orbit word of
  value \(P'>P\) with
  \[
    P'\equiv P\pmod {P^2R}.
  \]
  Hence \(P'=PQ\), where \(Q>1\) and \(Q\equiv1\pmod {PR}\). In
  particular, \(Q\) is coprime to the root and to every earlier edge
  quotient, and no prime up to the prescribed threshold divides \(Q\).

  Recursion defines \(\Phi\) on every node. The value at a node is the
  root value times the product of the edge quotients along its path.
  Since the root and all distinct edge quotients are pairwise coprime,
  a node value divides another exactly when its path-edge set is an
  initial segment of the other path. This is precisely the prefix
  relation and also proves injectivity.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Prime immunity for recurrent numeration systems]
  \label{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity}
  Let \((U,A,\mathcal R_U)\) satisfy \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, and put
  \[
    \mathcal P_U
      :=\{w\in\mathcal R_U:\val_U(w)\text{ is prime}\}.
  \]
  Then \(\mathcal P_U\) is MCF-immune, and therefore CF-immune,
  REG-immune, and not recognizable by a finite automaton over \(A\).

  If, in addition, \(\abs{a_0}=1\), then every infinite multiple
  context-free
  \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) has unbounded
  \(\omega(\val_U(w))\). More precisely, \(L\) contains a
  coprime-quotient chain \(N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i\) with
  \(Q_i>1\) and \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i}\). Thus the bounded-\(\omega\),
  bounded-\(\Omega\), and finite-prime-support languages of a unit
  system are MCF-immune whenever they are infinite. Without the unit
  hypothesis, every bounded-\(\Omega\) language is still MCF-immune.
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  The MCF-immunity assertions follow from
  \Cref{thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy}, including its
  bounded-\(\Omega\) conclusion. In a unit system \(S\) is empty, so
  that theorem gives unbounded \(\omega\). Taking
  \(M_i=r_i=1\) in
  \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain} gives the stated chain.
  Regular languages are context-free and context-free languages are
  MCFLs. Finally, \textup{(U2)} and Euclid's theorem make
  \(\mathcal P_U\) infinite.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Linear Pisot \(U\)-systems]
  \label{cor:linear-pisot-prime-cf-immunity}
  Let \(U=(U_n)_{n\ge0}\) be a linear Pisot \(U\)-system in the
  following precise sense: \(U_0=1\), the \(U_n\) are strictly increasing
  integers with bounded quotients, \(\mathcal R_U\) is the LSD-first
  reversal of the usual greedy representation language, and \(U\)
  eventually satisfies an integral recurrence whose characteristic
  polynomial is the minimal polynomial of a Pisot number. Then its prime
  representation language is MCF-immune, hence CF-immune, REG-immune and

 succeeded in 222ms:
  \]
  The factorization, and hence the orbit \(W(t)\), is independent of
  \(q\).
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Delete the finitely many words of length less than \(J\). Since the
  alphabet is finite, some \(a\in A^J\) is the prefix of infinitely many
  remaining words. MCFLs are closed, without increasing fan-out, under
  left quotient by a fixed word \cite[Section~3]{SekiEtAl1991}; hence
  \[
    L_a=\{x\in A^\ast:ax\in L\}
  \]
  is an infinite \(k\)-MCFL. The weak pumping lemma supplies
  \(u_j,v_j,w_j,s_j\) with the displayed synchronized pumping property
  and with at least one \(v_j\) or \(s_j\) nonempty.

  All digit actions after \(a\) are in the stationary range of
  \textup{(U4)}. Fix an allowed modulus \(q\). By
  \Cref{lem:affine-recurrence-block-action}, every nonempty matrix among
  \[
    M_{v_1},M_{s_1},\ldots,M_{v_k},M_{s_k}
  \]
  lies in the finite group
  \(\operatorname{GL}_{d+1}(\ZZ/q\ZZ)\). Let \(H(q)\) be a common
  multiple of their orders; empty blocks contribute the identity.
  Replacing \(t\) by \(t+H(q)\) in the matrix product for \(W(t)\)
  therefore leaves every powered block unchanged modulo \(q\), proving
  the congruence simultaneously for all \(t\ge0\).

  Finally,
  \[
    \abs{W(t+1)}-\abs{W(t)}
      =\sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0.
  \]
  Both words are canonical, so \textup{(U3)} gives
  \(N(t+1)>N(t)\).
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Explicit grammar for a synchronized ray]
  \label{lem:synchronized-ray-linear-mcfg}
  Fix \(k\ge1\), a word \(a\), and words
  \(u_1,\ldots,u_{k+1},v_1,\ldots,v_k,w_1,\ldots,w_k,
  s_1,\ldots,s_k\) over a finite alphabet. If
  \[
    \sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0,
  \]
  then
  \[
    \left\{a u_1v_1^tw_1s_1^tu_2\cdots
      u_kv_k^tw_ks_k^tu_{k+1}:t\ge0\right\}
  \]
  is an infinite linear \(2k\)-MCFL.
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  Take a nonterminal \(X\) of fan-out \(2k\) and a start symbol \(T\) of
  fan-out one. The productions are
  \[
    X(\varepsilon,\ldots,\varepsilon)\leftarrow,
  \]
  \[
    X(v_1x_1,s_1x_2,\ldots,v_kx_{2k-1},s_kx_{2k})
      \leftarrow X(x_1,\ldots,x_{2k}),
  \]
  and
  \[
    T(a u_1x_1w_1x_2u_2\cdots
      u_kx_{2k-1}w_kx_{2k}u_{k+1})
      \leftarrow X(x_1,\ldots,x_{2k}).
  \]
  After \(t\) recursive applications, the tuple derived from \(X\) is
  \((v_1^t,s_1^t,\ldots,v_k^t,s_k^t)\), so the generated language is
  exactly the displayed ray. Every variable occurs exactly once in each
  production. The positive total pumped length makes the words at
  distinct parameters have distinct lengths.
\end{proof}

Put \(S=\{p:p\text{ is prime and }p\mid a_0\}\). On
\(\NN_{\ge1}\), let \(\tau_S\) be the topology generated by the basis
\[
  \{(n+q\ZZ)\cap\NN_{\ge1}:n\ge1,\ q\ge1,\ \gcd(q,a_0)=1\}.
\]
Its completion is
\[
  \widehat{\ZZ}_{S^c}
    :=\varprojlim_{\gcd(q,a_0)=1}\ZZ/q\ZZ
    \cong\prod_{p\notin S}\ZZ_p.
\]
These are the adic topologies and completions studied by Broughan
\cite{Broughan2003}.

\begin{theorem}[MCFL local-adic topological fingerprint]
  \label{thm:mcfl-local-adic-fingerprint}
  For the orbit \(Y=\{N(t):t\ge0\}\) supplied by
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, the following hold.
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(\roman*)}]
    \item The subspace \(Y\subseteq(\NN_{\ge1},\tau_S)\) has no isolated
    points and is homeomorphic to \(\mathbb Q\).
    \item Its closure in \(\widehat{\ZZ}_{S^c}\) is homeomorphic to the
    Cantor set.
    \item If \(B\subseteq\NN_{\ge1}\) is scattered in \(\tau_S\), then
    \[
      \operatorname{Rep}_U(B)
        :=\{w\in\mathcal R_U:\val_U(w)\in B\}
    \]
    is MCF-immune whenever it is infinite.
  \end{enumerate}
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Let \(N(t)\in Y\), and let its basic neighborhood be specified modulo
  an allowed \(q\). If \(q=1\), take any later orbit point. If \(q\ge2\),
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit} gives
  \(N(t+H(q))\equiv N(t)\pmod q\), and strict increase makes these points
  distinct. Thus \(Y\) has no isolated point.

  The allowed congruences separate distinct positive integers: for
  \(m\ne n\), choose a prime outside the finite set \(S\) that does not
  divide \(m-n\). Consequently \(\tau_S\) is Hausdorff and metrizable;
  it also has a countable basis. Hence \(Y\) is a countable metrizable
  space without isolated points. The Sierpi\'nski characterization used
  in \cite[Theorem~2.4]{Broughan2003} yields \(Y\cong\mathbb Q\).

  The profinite ring \(\widehat{\ZZ}_{S^c}\) is compact, metrizable, and
  zero-dimensional. Its closed subspace \(\overline Y\) has no isolated
  point: an isolated point of the closure would, by density of \(Y\),
  belong to and be isolated in \(Y\). Thus \(\overline Y\) is a nonempty
  compact perfect zero-dimensional metrizable space, and the classical
  Cantor-space characterization gives the second assertion; compare
  \cite[Corollary~3.1]{Broughan2003}.

  For the last assertion, an infinite MCFL sublanguage of
  \(\operatorname{Rep}_U(B)\) would, by the first assertion, provide a
  nonempty subspace \(Y\subseteq B\) without isolated points. This
  contradicts scatteredness, which is hereditary to subspaces.
\end{proof}

For a prime \(p\), write \(v_p(n)\) for the exponent of \(p\) in \(n\),
and let \(\omega_{S^c}(n)=\#\{p\notin S:p\mid n\}\).
For \(K\ge0\) and \(E=(E_p)_{p\in S}\in\NN_0^S\), define
\[
  X_{K,E}=\{n\ge1:\omega_{S^c}(n)\le K,
               \ v_p(n)\le E_p\ (p\in S)\}.
\]
For \(X\subseteq\NN_{\ge1}\), let \(D_SX\) be the set of points of \(X\)
that are not isolated in its \(\tau_S\)-subspace topology. Thus \(D_S\)
is the intrinsic Cantor--Bendixson derivative.

\begin{proposition}[Exact local prime-factor rank]
  \label{prop:local-prime-factor-cb-rank}
  For every \(K\ge1\),
  \[
    D_SX_{K,E}=X_{K-1,E}.
  \]
  Consequently
  \[
    D_S^{\,j}X_{K,E}=X_{K-j,E}\quad(0\le j\le K),
    \qquad D_S^{\,K+1}X_{K,E}=\varnothing,
  \]
  and the Cantor--Bendixson height of \(X_{K,E}\) is exactly \(K+1\).
\end{proposition}

\begin{proof}
  Suppose first that \(n\in X_{K-1,E}\), and let \(q\ge1\) be coprime
  to \(a_0\). Dirichlet's theorem supplies a prime
  \(r\equiv1\pmod q\) outside the finite set \(S\cup\{p:p\mid n\}\).
  Then \(nr\ne n\), \(nr\equiv n\pmod q\), the \(S\)-adic valuations
  are unchanged, and
  \[
    \omega_{S^c}(nr)=\omega_{S^c}(n)+1\le K.
  \]
  Hence \(n\in D_SX_{K,E}\).

  Conversely, let \(n\in X_{K,E}\) have
  \(\omega_{S^c}(n)=K\), and write
  \[
    n=c\prod_{i=1}^K\ell_i^{e_i},
    \qquad c=\prod_{p\in S}p^{f_p},\quad 0\le f_p\le E_p,
  \]
  with distinct \(\ell_i\notin S\). Put
  \(q_0=\prod_i\ell_i^{e_i+1}\). If \(m\in X_{K,E}\) and
  \(m\equiv n\pmod {q_0}\), then
  \(v_{\ell_i}(m)=e_i\) for every \(i\). The budget of \(K\) primes
  outside \(S\) leaves no further such prime, so \(m\) belongs to the
  finite set
  \[
    F=\left\{
      \left(\prod_{p\in S}p^{g_p}\right)
      \prod_{i=1}^K\ell_i^{e_i}:
      0\le g_p\le E_p
    \right\}.
  \]
  Choose a prime \(r\notin S\cup\{\ell_1,\ldots,\ell_K\}\) dividing
  none of the finitely many nonzero differences \(m-n\), \(m\in F\).
  Then \(q=q_0r\) is coprime to \(a_0\), and the \(q\)-neighborhood of
  \(n\) meets \(X_{K,E}\) only at \(n\). Thus precisely the points with
  fewer than \(K\) outside primes survive one derivative. Iteration
  proves the formula. Finally \(X_{0,E}\) is a nonempty finite set (it
  contains \(1\)), so the height is exactly \(K+1\).
\end{proof}

Broughan's related full-adic result counts prime factors with
multiplicity and identifies closures of the corresponding strata
\cite[Theorem~4.3]{Broughan2003}. The preceding proposition instead
uses distinct prime support, deletes the finite set \(S\), imposes
bounded \(S\)-adic valuations, and computes the intrinsic derivative
rank.

\begin{theorem}[Nonunit escape dichotomy and MCF-immunity]
  \label{thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy}
  Let \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) be an infinite MCFL. Then either
  \[
    \sup_{w\in L}\omega_{S^c}(\val_U(w))=\infty,
  \]
  or there is a prime \(p\mid a_0\) such that
  \[
    \sup_{w\in L}v_p(\val_U(w))=\infty.
  \]
  Consequently the following languages are MCF-immune whenever they are

 succeeded in 238ms:
\section{Context-free pumping and Pisot recurrence actions}
\label{sec:context-free-pisot}

We now pass from finite automata to pushdown automata. Recall that an
infinite language is \emph{CF-immune} if it contains no infinite
context-free sublanguage. Here a context-free language means one accepted
by an arbitrary nondeterministic pushdown automaton, equivalently generated
by a context-free grammar. We impose no determinism, unambiguity,
real-time, or growth hypothesis on that automaton. The classical
fixed-base theorem is due independently to Hartmanis--Shank and
Sch\"utzenberger
\cite{HartmanisShank1968,Schutzenberger1968}. The point below is that
the square-modulus block action survives the two simultaneous pumps
supplied by Ogden's lemma \cite{Ogden1968}; the interchange lemma of
Ogden--Ross--Winklmann \cite{OgdenRossWinklmann1985} is a stronger
related tool, but is not needed for the proof.

\begin{lemma}[Paired Zeckendorf pumping congruence]
  \label{lem:zeckendorf-paired-pumping-congruence}
  Let \(u,v,x,y,z\in\{0,1\}^\ast\), and put
  \[
    N(k):=\val(uv^kxy^kz)\qquad(k\ge0).
  \]
  Suppose that \(\abs v+\abs y>0\). If \(q\ge2\), and \(h\) is a
  common multiple of the orders modulo \(q\) of the nonempty-block
  matrices among \(M_v,M_y\) (with \(M_\varepsilon=I\)), then
  \[
    N(k+h)\equiv N(k)\pmod q
    \qquad(k\ge0).
  \]
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  With \(v_0=(0,1,1)^t\), concatenation and
  \Cref{lem:zeckendorf-unimodular-block-action} give
  \[
    N(k)=e_1^tM_zM_y^kM_xM_v^kM_uv_0.
  \]
  Every nonempty block matrix is invertible modulo \(q\). By the
  definition of \(h\), both \(M_v^h\equiv I\pmod q\) and
  \(M_y^h\equiv I\pmod q\), where the same identities are automatic for
  an empty block. Substitution in the displayed product proves the
  congruence.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Context-free coprime-quotient Zeckendorf pumping]
  \label{thm:context-free-coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}
  Let \(L\subseteq\mathcal Z\) be context-free. There is an integer
  \(P_L\ge1\) such that every \(w\in L\) with
  \(\abs w\ge P_L\) and \(\val(w)\ge2\) begins a sequence
  \(w_0=w,w_1,w_2,\ldots\) in \(L\) whose lengths are strictly
  increasing and whose values \(N_i=\val(w_i)\) satisfy
  \[
    N_{i+1}=N_iQ_i,\qquad Q_i>1,\qquad
    Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i},\qquad \gcd(Q_i,N_i)=1.
  \]
\end{theorem}

\begin{proof}
  Apply Ogden's lemma with every position distinguished. It supplies a
  constant \(P_L\) such that each \(w\in L\) of length at least \(P_L\)
  has a factorisation
  \[
    w=uvxyz,\qquad \abs v+\abs y>0,\qquad
    uv^kxy^kz\in L\quad(k\ge0).
  \]
  The additional marked-position bound in Ogden's lemma is not needed
  here. Starting with \(w_i\in L\), choose such a factorisation and
  let \(h_i\) be a common multiple of the orders of \(M_v\) and \(M_y\)
  in
  \(\operatorname{GL}_3(\ZZ/N_i^2\ZZ)\), omitting an empty block. These
  orders exist by
  \Cref{lem:zeckendorf-unimodular-block-action}. Define
  \[
    w_{i+1}:=uv^{\,1+h_i}xy^{\,1+h_i}z.
  \]
  By \Cref{lem:zeckendorf-paired-pumping-congruence},
  \(N_{i+1}\equiv N_i\pmod {N_i^2}\). Hence
  \(N_{i+1}=N_i(1+t_iN_i)\) for an integer \(t_i\).

  Since \(h_i\ge1\) and \(\abs v+\abs y>0\), the new word is longer.
  Both words belong to \(L\subseteq\mathcal Z\), and canonical
  Zeckendorf intervals are strictly ordered by length; consequently
  \(N_{i+1}>N_i\), so \(t_i\ge1\). Taking
  \(Q_i=1+t_iN_i\) gives all four assertions. The new word still has
  length at least \(P_L\) and value at least \(2\), so the construction
  iterates.
\end{proof}

\begin{corollary}[Zeckendorf CF-immunity package]
  \label{cor:zeckendorf-context-free-immunity-package}
  Every infinite context-free \(L\subseteq\mathcal Z\) satisfies
  \[
    \sup_{w\in L}\omega(\val(w))=\infty.
  \]
  Consequently, for each \(K\ge1\), the bounded-distinct-prime language
  \(\mathcal B_K\), the bounded-almost-prime language \(\mathcal A_K\),
  and every infinite finite-prime-support language \(\mathcal Z_S\)
  are CF-immune. In particular, the Zeckendorf prime language
  \(\mathcal P_{\mathcal Z}\) is CF-immune.
\end{corollary}

\begin{proof}
  Choose a sufficiently long word in \(L\) and apply
  \Cref{thm:context-free-coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}. Since
  \(N_j\mid N_i\) for \(j\le i\), while every \(Q_i>1\) is coprime to
  \(N_i\), a prime divisor of \(Q_i\) divides no earlier value. Thus
  \(\omega(N_i)\ge\omega(N_0)+i\). The immunity conclusions follow
  exactly as in the regular cases: \(\omega\le K\) on \(\mathcal B_K\),
  \(\mathcal A_K\subseteq\mathcal B_K\), and
  \(\omega\le\abs S\) on \(\mathcal Z_S\). Finally prime values have
  \(\omega=1\), while Euclid's theorem makes
  \(\mathcal P_{\mathcal Z}\) infinite.
\end{proof}

We next isolate the precise base-independent interface. This is
deliberately narrower than every object called a Pisot or abstract
numeration system in the literature.

\begin{definition}[Canonical eventually recurrent numeration]
  \label{def:canonical-eventually-recurrent-numeration}
  A canonical eventually recurrent numeration system consists of:
  \begin{enumerate}[label=\textup{(U\arabic*)}]
    \item a strictly increasing sequence of positive integers
    \(U=(U_n)_{n\ge0}\) and a finite digit alphabet
    \(A\subseteq\NN\) containing \(0\);
    \item an LSD-first language \(\mathcal R_U\subseteq A^\ast\), with
    \[
      \val_U(x_0\cdots x_{m-1})
        :=\sum_{j=0}^{m-1}x_jU_j,
    \]
    for which \(\val_U:\mathcal R_U\to\NN_{\ge1}\) is a bijection;
    \item strict length ordering: if \(r,s\in\mathcal R_U\) and
    \(\abs r<\abs s\), then \(\val_U(r)<\val_U(s)\);
    \item integers \(J\ge0,d\ge1,a_0,\ldots,a_{d-1}\), with
    \(a_0\ne0\), such that
    \[
      U_{n+d}=a_0U_n+a_1U_{n+1}+\cdots+a_{d-1}U_{n+d-1}
      \qquad(n\ge J).
    \]
  \end{enumerate}
  Relative to this recurrence witness, it is a \emph{unit system} if
  \(\abs{a_0}=1\). No regularity of \(\mathcal R_U\) is included in
  this definition.
\end{definition}

\begin{lemma}[Affine recurrence block action]
  \label{lem:affine-recurrence-block-action}
  Under \textup{(U4)}, let \(C\) be the \(d\times d\) companion matrix
  with superdiagonal entries \(1\) and last row
  \((a_0,\ldots,a_{d-1})\). For \(\varepsilon\in A\), put
  \[
    D_\varepsilon=
    \begin{pmatrix}
      1&\varepsilon e_1^t\\
      0&C
    \end{pmatrix}.
  \]
  Once \(r\ge J\), appending \(\varepsilon\) sends
  \[
    (N_r,U_r,\ldots,U_{r+d-1})^t
    \quad\hbox{to}\quad
    D_\varepsilon(N_r,U_r,\ldots,U_{r+d-1})^t.
  \]
  Moreover
  \(\det D_\varepsilon=(-1)^{d-1}a_0\). Hence every block action is
  invertible modulo \(q\) whenever \(\gcd(q,a_0)=1\), and modulo every
  \(q\ge2\) in a unit system. Here and below, for a block
  \(b=b_1\cdots b_\ell\), we write
  \(M_b=D_{b_\ell}\cdots D_{b_1}\), with \(M_\varepsilon=I\).
\end{lemma}

\begin{proof}
  The first coordinate becomes \(N_r+\varepsilon U_r\); the remaining
  coordinates shift once and the final one is supplied by \textup{(U4)}.
  These are exactly the displayed matrix equations. The determinant
  is \(\det C=(-1)^{d-1}a_0\). Multiplicativity of determinants gives
  the assertion for blocks.
\end{proof}

The coefficient \(a_0\) belongs to the displayed recurrence witness,
not intrinsically to \((U,A,\mathcal R_U)\); see
\Cref{prop:recurrence-witness-separation} for the resulting strict
separation.

We now use the standard fan-out convention: a multiple context-free
language (MCFL) is a \(k\)-MCFL for some finite \(k\ge1\). An infinite
language is \emph{MCF-immune} if it contains no infinite MCFL
sublanguage. The weak pumping lemma of Seki--Matsumura--Fujii--Kasami
states that an infinite \(k\)-MCFL contains one word that can be pumped
synchronously at \(2k\) factors \cite[Lemma~3.2]{SekiEtAl1991}. The
lemma makes no assertion about every sufficiently long word; that
stronger form fails for general MCFLs
\cite{KanazawaKobeleMichaelisSalvatiYoshinaka2014}.

\begin{theorem}[Synchronized local-congruence orbit]
  \label{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}
  Let \((U,A,\mathcal R_U)\) satisfy \textup{(U1)--(U4)}, and let
  \(L\subseteq\mathcal R_U\) be an infinite \(k\)-MCFL. There are words
  \[
    \begin{gathered}
      a,u_1,\ldots,u_{k+1},v_1,\ldots,v_k\in A^\ast,\\
      w_1,\ldots,w_k,s_1,\ldots,s_k\in A^\ast
    \end{gathered}
  \]
  such that
  \[
    W(t)=a u_1v_1^tw_1s_1^tu_2\cdots
          u_kv_k^tw_ks_k^tu_{k+1}\in L
          \qquad(t\ge0),
  \]
  \[
    \sum_{j=1}^k(\abs{v_j}+\abs{s_j})>0,
  \]
  and \(N(t):=\val_U(W(t))\) is strictly increasing. Moreover, for
  every \(q\ge2\) with \(\gcd(q,a_0)=1\), there is \(H(q)\ge1\) such
  that
  \[
    N(t+H(q))\equiv N(t)\pmod q
    \qquad\text{for every }t\ge0.

\section{Further remarks}\label{sec:conclusion}

This paper proves a finite-budget obstruction for fixed finite-state
models facing prime-scale slices. The effective fixed-DFA theorem makes
the base-\(b\) obstruction computable from the transition matrix: the
residue alternatives, thresholds, and constants come from finite
Markov-chain linear algebra plus explicit prime-counting bounds. The
multicomponent presentation theorem keeps the residue-wise polynomial
factors on \(\lambda\)-active classes of arbitrary trim deterministic
presentations and compares those exponents against targets of size
\(\asymp\lambda^m/m\).

Together these results show that fixed-DFA approximation of prime
slices fails quantitatively: recall and precision cannot stay
simultaneously bounded below, and the symmetric difference from the
prime slice is always at least \(c_{\mathcal A,b}b^m/m\) in the ordinary
base-\(b\) setting and at least
\(c_{\mathcal A,\varphi}\varphi^m/m\) in the Zeckendorf setting. The
  Zeckendorf section also proves a sharper PNT-free language-theoretic
  obstruction. The unimodular action modulo \(N_i^2\) works for the two
  simultaneous pumps supplied by Ogden's lemma and gives the stated
  context-free coprime-quotient chain.

The same calculation depends only on a stationary affine companion
action. Under unique canonical representation, strict length ordering,
and an eventual integral recurrence with nonzero trailing coefficient,
the weak MCFL pumping lemma supplies one increasing orbit that is
periodic modulo every integer coprime to the trailing coefficient. Its
deleted-congruence subspace is a copy of \(\mathbb Q\), and its closure
in the corresponding profinite completion is a Cantor set. The exact
Cantor--Bendixson rank of bounded local prime-factor layers gives the
recurrence-witness-relative nonunit dichotomy: every infinite MCFL has
unbounded prime support away from the trailing coefficient or unbounded
valuation at one of its prime divisors. Thus prime and bounded-\(\Omega\) languages are
MCF-immune for all systems in the recurrent interface.

The selected trailing coefficient need not be intrinsic. In particular,
inflating the Fibonacci recurrence by \(X-p\) produces the
same selected bad-prime set as base \(p\) without producing its escape
language.

For the intrinsic minimal tail recurrence, the residual escape question
has an exact structural answer. An infinite MCFL with bounded prime
support away from the minimal constant term exists if and only if the
canonical language contains a synchronized pumping ray whose numerical
values are \(cb^t\) with integers \(c\ge1\) and \(b\ge2\). Such a ray has
an explicit finite-fan-out linear MCFG. With a DFA for the canonical
syntax, candidate rays can be enumerated and checked by finite-monoid
periodicity and exact recurrence identities, giving positive
semidecidability. Termination on negative instances, and hence general
decidability, remains open.

In a unit recurrence, the same fixed orbit realizes quotient
congruences of arbitrary prescribed depth. A recursive use of these
congruences embeds the full countably branching tree as an induced
divisibility suborder, with pairwise coprime edge quotients whose least
prime factors exceed arbitrary edge-wise thresholds. Standard linear
Pisot \(U\)-systems satisfy the recurrent interface; Frougny
normalization supplies their finite-state canonical syntax. In the
nonunit case, ordinary base-\(b\) powers show that the valuation escape
alternative is necessary. The final analytic appendix is only
contextual comparison.

Several natural questions remain outside the scope of this paper. The
density and finite-budget results use determinism essentially;
nondeterministic, weighted, or
bounded-ambiguity variants would require new hypotheses because
cancellation or ambiguity can change the counting problem. Broader
arithmetic targets other than primes are likewise future-work
directions rather than theorem claims here. Arbitrary real-base
\(\beta\)-shifts, redundant representations, and non-positional
abstract numeration systems also remain outside the theorem unless they
can be equipped with the stationary invertible block action isolated in
\Cref{rem:pisot-open-interfaces}.

  \]
  The bounded-distinct-prime and finite-prime-support REG-immunity
  corollaries are the main language-theoretic consequences; bounded
  almost-prime and prime-language REG-immunity are weaker downstream
  cases. The slice-rigidity statement
  \(\Cref{prop:zeckendorf-regular-slice-rigidity}\) remains a separate
  density-scale obstruction, while the nonregularity corollary
  \(\Cref{cor:zeckendorf-prime-language-not-regular}\) follows
  immediately from REG-immunity. The sofic-slice exclusion
  \(\Cref{rem:zeckendorf-primes-not-sofic}\) is then a short consequence
  of regularity of sofic block languages.

  \item \textbf{Context-free, MCFL, and Pisot extensions.}
  Fixed-base prime CF-immunity is classical. Hartmanis and Shank proved
  that no infinite set of primes can be accepted in fixed-base notation
  by a pushdown automaton; Sch\"utzenberger obtained the conclusion
  independently \cite{HartmanisShank1968,Schutzenberger1968}. We do not
  reproduce their proofs as new. Our use of Ogden's lemma
  \cite{Ogden1968} instead couples the two pumped blocks to the
  Zeckendorf unimodular action and yields the stronger coprime-quotient
  chain
  \Cref{thm:context-free-coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}. The
  weak pumping lemma of Seki--Matsumura--Fujii--Kasami
  \cite{SekiEtAl1991} supplies one synchronized \(2k\)-block orbit for
  every infinite \(k\)-MCFL; it is used as an input, not reproved.
  Kanazawa--Kobele--Michaelis--Salvati--Yoshinaka prove that the usual
  strong pumping form fails in general
  \cite{KanazawaKobeleMichaelisSalvatiYoshinaka2014}, and the recent
  substitution lemma of Duncan--Elder--Frenkel--Liu gives a different
  strong interface \cite{DuncanElderFrenkelLiu2026}. Neither result
  concerns recurrence numeration or divisibility. The new deductions
  are the fixed local-congruence orbit
  \Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit}, the topological
  fingerprint \Cref{thm:mcfl-local-adic-fingerprint}, the exact rank
  formula \Cref{prop:local-prime-factor-cb-rank}, and the escape
  dichotomy \Cref{thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy}. The unit-system
  constructions are \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain} and
  \Cref{thm:unit-mcfl-divisibility-tree}.
  Evertse's theorem on prime ideals in ratios of terms of
  nondegenerate recurrences \cite{Evertse1984} is used as an arithmetic
  input to the geometric-ray characterization
  \Cref{thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray}; it is not claimed as
  a new recurrence theorem.
  Broughan's \(\mathbb Q\)- and Cantor-space classifications for adic
  integer topologies are cited as classical inputs
  \cite{Broughan2003}. Frougny normalization and the
  Bertrand/substitution literature
  \cite{Frougny1992,BruyereHansel1997,Fabre1995,FrougnySolomyak1992}
  supply the canonical finite-state numeration interface, not the
  arithmetic pumping proof. The residual nonunit and non-positional
  limits are stated in \Cref{rem:pisot-open-interfaces}. The
  recurrence-witness separation
  \Cref{prop:recurrence-witness-separation} distinguishes the invariant
  minimal action from an arbitrarily inflated companion presentation.

  \item \textbf{Analytic obstructions: classical background.}
  \Cref{thm:euler-product-natural-boundary} is the usual
  P\'olya--Carlson natural-boundary mechanism for Euler products.
  \Cref{prop:finite-zeta-imaginary-periodicity} is the corresponding
  periodicity obstruction for determinant models after \(z=e^{-s}\). Both are
  classical
  \cite{Polya1923,Carlson1921,Estermann1928,ArtinMazur1965,BowenLanford1970}.
\end{itemize}
\smallskip
\noindent\textbf{Relation to density and approximation literature.} For ordinary
automatic sets, asymptotic density questions go back at least to
Berstel \cite{Berstel1973}; the standard global sources include
B\"uchi's automata-theoretic decidability work \cite{Buchi1960} and
Allouche--Shallit's monograph on automatic sequences
\cite{AlloucheShallit2003}. Matrix and algorithmic treatments appear
in
\cite{BodirskyGaertnerVonOertzenSchwinghammer2004,Bell2020UpperDensity},
and Koga's paper \cite{Koga2019Density} revisits Sin'ya's density-zero
criterion \cite{Sinya2021}. The rational-measure viewpoint of Hansel
and Perrin \cite{HanselPerrin1989} interprets regular languages via
probability measures on shifts of finite type. Kozik's MFCS 2005 paper
\cite{Kozik2005Conditional} formulates conditional densities for
deterministic presentations in the trim strongly connected setting,
Montoya \cite{Montoya2025Relative} recasts these questions in terms of
relative densities, and Berth\'e--Goulet-Ouellet--Perrin
\cite{BertheGouletPerrin2025} establish a broad density theorem under
coprime-quotient pumping theorem
\Cref{thm:coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}. Its principal
consequences are
\Cref{cor:unbounded-distinct-prime-divisors,cor:bounded-distinct-prime-zeckendorf-languages-reg-immune}
and \Cref{cor:finite-prime-support-zeckendorf-languages-reg-immune};
\Cref{prop:zeckendorf-regular-slice-rigidity} remains a separate
slice-cardinality obstruction, and
\Cref{cor:zeckendorf-prime-language-not-regular} is an immediate
consequence of REG-immunity.
Context-free Zeckendorf pumping is
\Cref{thm:context-free-coprime-quotient-zeckendorf-pumping}, with the
immunity consequences in
\Cref{cor:zeckendorf-context-free-immunity-package}. The stronger MCFL
package is
\Cref{thm:mcfl-synchronized-congruence-orbit,thm:mcfl-local-adic-fingerprint,prop:local-prime-factor-cb-rank,thm:nonunit-mcfl-escape-dichotomy,thm:bounded-outside-support-geometric-ray,cor:bounded-outside-support-semidecidable,thm:unit-mcfl-deep-congruence-chain,thm:unit-mcfl-divisibility-tree};
its linear Pisot specialization is
\Cref{thm:recurrent-numeration-prime-cf-immunity,cor:linear-pisot-prime-cf-immunity}.
The eventual-sofic exclusion is recorded only as the brief consequence
\Cref{rem:zeckendorf-primes-not-sofic}, while
\Cref{thm:euler-product-natural-boundary,prop:finite-zeta-imaginary-periodicity}
remain classical background.

\medskip
Finite-automata non-recognizability of the base-\(b\) prime language
goes back to Minsky and Papert \cite{MinskyPapert1966}.
Allen \cite{Allen1968}, Hartmanis--Shank
\cite{HartmanisShank1968}, and Sch\"utzenberger
\cite{Schutzenberger1968} proved stronger related results for broader
language classes and acceptable sets. Shallit's survey and monograph
treat automaticity and number theory from the finite-automata viewpoint
\cite{Shallit1996,AlloucheShallit2003}. Recent work of Yuen and Dubbe
studies regularity and quantitative automaticity around the prime set
\cite{Yuen2022,Dubbe2024}, and Shen proves that prime automata in Yao's
finite-automata primeness model do not exist \cite{Shen2022}. Those
results concern
recognizability or growing-state quantitative automaticity. The present
theorems are different: the automaton is fixed, the comparison is made
on each fixed length slice, and the conclusion is a recall/precision
or symmetric-difference obstruction rather than a state lower bound for
a sequence of automata.

The Zeckendorf theorem in \Cref{sec:zeckendorf} should be compared with,
but not identified with, the classical base-representation results of
Sch\"utzenberger. In a fixed base the digit value update is controlled
by multiplication by the base modulo a prime. For LSD-first Zeckendorf
words the corresponding update is instead the unimodular \(3\times3\)
matrix action on
\((\val(\text{prefix}),F_{r+1},F_{r+2})^t\). This invertibility modulo
the square of the current value is what makes the pumping congruence
work and yields quotients \(Q_i\equiv1\pmod {N_i}\). Thus the quotient
is coprime to the previous value, forcing new rational prime divisors;
the prime-language REG-immunity is the \(K=1\) bounded-distinct-prime
subcase.

The interaction between primes and digital
constraints has a much deeper literature. Mauduit and Rivat
\cite{MauduitRivat2010}, Drmota--Mauduit--Rivat
\cite{DrmotaMauduitRivat2009}, and M\"ullner \cite{Muellner2017} prove
analytic equidistribution results for digit functions and automatic
sequences evaluated at primes. Maynard's theorem on primes with missing
digits \cite{Maynard2019}, Bourgain's work on primes with prescribed
binary digits
\cite{Bourgain2013PrescribedDigits,Bourgain2015PrescribedDigitsII},
Swaenepoel's extension to a positive proportion of preassigned digits
in arbitrary bases \cite{Swaenepoel2020PreassignedDigits}, and
Bhowmik--Suzuki's work on reversed primes in arithmetic progressions
\cite{BhowmikSuzuki2024} address complementary problems about primes
and digital restrictions. These analytic results allow the digit
constraints to vary with the length and prove prime
asymptotics or positive proportions inside those varying families. The
present paper does neither: the automaton is fixed once and for all,
and the obstruction is the elementary mismatch between eventual
periodic finite-state densities and the finite prime budget
\(\asymp\lambda^m/m\). For Zeckendorf and more general Parry numeration
systems, the uniqueness theorem goes back to Lekkerkerker and
Zeckendorf \cite{Lekkerkerker1952,Zeckendorf1972}, and the regularity
of admissible expansions is standard; see
\cite{Frougny1992,BruyereHansel1997,Rigo2014Vol1,Rigo2014Vol2}. Analytic
work of Drmota--M\"ullner--Spiegelhofer on primes in Fibonacci or
Zeckendorf representations \cite{DrmotaMuellnerSpiegelhofer2021}
studies prime distribution under numeration constraints. Here we use
only the size of the Zeckendorf interval and regular syntax, not an
equidistribution theorem for Zeckendorf digit patterns. The
transfer-matrix viewpoint on word counts is familiar from symbolic
dynamics and analytic combinatorics
\cite{LindMarcus1995,Kitchens1998,FlajoletSedgewick2009}.

Recent Monatshefte work illustrates the surrounding symbolic-dynamical
landscape: Bustos-Gajardo--Kellendonk--Yassawi use finite semigroup
methods for factors of substitution shifts
\cite{BustosKellendonkYassawi2025}, and Charlier--Cisternino study
soficity phenomena in Cantor real-base expansions
\cite{CharlierCisternino2021}. These are complementary to the present
finite-budget obstruction; they do not supply, and are not used as, a
prime-slice density theorem.

For clarity, the comparison with prior work can be summarized as follows.
Regular-language density results of
\cite{HanselPerrin1989,Kozik2005Conditional,Koga2019Density,Montoya2025Relative,BertheGouletPerrin2025}
give the periodic or conditional density statements used here; the new
increment is to feed the resulting residue constants into explicit
prime-slice thresholds for one fixed automaton. Numeration-system work
\cite{Frougny1992,BruyereHansel1997,CharlierRampersad2011,Rigo2014Vol1,Rigo2014Vol2}
supplies regular admissible languages and growth laws for abstract
numeration, including Zeckendorf-type systems; in the trim strongly
connected cases stated explicitly, the present paper transfers the same
fixed-prime-slice obstruction to Zeckendorf length slices with computable
constants. For primes in Fibonacci or Zeckendorf representations, we use
only the prime number theorem estimate
\(\#\{p\in[F_{m+1},F_{m+2})\}\asymp\varphi^m/m\), and no distribution
theorem for Zeckendorf digit patterns is claimed.

The sofic and orbit-growth papers
\cite{BustosKellendonkYassawi2025,CharlierCisternino2021}, together with
the classical zeta references recalled in \Cref{sec:analytic}, are used
only for contextual comparison: the present argument separates those
phenomena from the fixed prime-slice density obstruction. Similarly,
regular-approximation frameworks for nonregular languages
\cite{EismanRavikumar2005,Sinya2021} position the problem, but the
obstruction proved here is slice-wise and quantitative for one fixed DFA,
not an optimal approximation scheme. The main theorem chain,
\Cref{thm:effective-fixed-dfa-prime-obstruction,thm:multicomponent-finite-budget,cor:relative-finite-budget-obstruction},
computes residue alternatives, thresholds, and constants for a fixed
finite automaton, and compares multicomponent polynomial exponents
against a prime budget of order \(\lambda^m/m\).

\subsection{Organisation}

\Cref{sec:automata} proves the density dichotomy for deterministic
automata and derives the fixed-base prime corollaries.
\Cref{sec:zeckendorf} records the background growth law for regular
Zeckendorf sublanguages, deduces the corresponding quantitative prime
corollaries, proves the PNT-free coprime-quotient Zeckendorf pumping
theorem and its unbounded-\(\omega\), bounded-distinct-prime,
finite-prime-support, and prime-language REG-immunity corollaries,
records the older slice-level
rigidity as a separate density obstruction, and then notes the easy
nonregularity and sofic exclusions.
\Cref{sec:context-free-pisot} proves the paired-pump Zeckendorf theorem,
the synchronized MCFL orbit, its local-adic and Cantor--Bendixson
consequences, the nonunit escape dichotomy, the geometric-ray
characterization and its positive semidecidability consequence, and the
unit-system divisibility tree, then specializes the recurrent interface
to linear Pisot \(U\)-systems.
\Cref{sec:analytic} collects final background analytic comparators
concerning Euler products and scalar determinant models. Later sections
cite the statements recorded above directly, so their proofs and
corollaries need not restate the results in full.
